# Windows fixed contracts

### 2026-10-30 — Ollama advertised thinking controls

- Both Cores select `think: false` only from an actual boolean false in cached /api/show thinking.values; otherwise use the lowest advertised low/medium/high string or omit think for unknown/absent controls. Capability alone never invents a control. Shared request goldens cover booleans, GPT-OSS levels in reverse order, medium minimum, unknown and absent metadata.

### 2026-10-30 — Gemini schema compatibility retained

- Keep responseMimeType/responseJsonSchema: the current generateContent reference deprecates the older field and describes responseFormat, but does not explicitly establish its general availability for all three curated models. Dated source comments link the reference; tests pin the existing schema field on each curated model without mixing the two formats. No speculative wire migration.

### 2026-10-30 — Gemini effort and total output budget

- Gemini 3+ maps canonical low/medium/high to LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH thinking levels; older/custom nonmatching ids omit the unsupported field. Shared request goldens cover all efforts, absent effort, legacy ids, connection tests and clip/tighten requests. The canonical cap is a combined thinking-plus-visible cap, never silently enlarged, and usage includes thoughts. Google documents relative thinking allowances, not a guaranteed visible-token reserve, and forbids combining thinkingLevel with thinkingBudget; no such guarantee is claimed.

### 2026-10-30 — Gemini terminal reasons

- Mirrored tables accept only `STOP` as complete. Token limits truncate; policy/image blocks and escalation refuse; PUP-limited accounts are unavailable; malformed, tool, missing, unspecified and future reasons fail closed as server errors without a corrective retry. Prompt blocking takes precedence. Both Core suites cover every documented FinishReason plus empty/unknown values.

### 2026-10-30 — OpenAI failed Responses

- Both Cores use the same documented `Response.error.code` table for HTTP-200 failed envelopes: rate limits, policy refusals, region unavailability, invalid-image requests and server failures. HTTP-only error codes are not reinterpreted here. Tests cover all 21 documented codes, unknown codes and HTTP-only codes; failed envelopes never trigger a transport or corrective retry.

### 2026-10-30 — Anthropic context exhaustion

- `model_context_window_exceeded` maps to the same truncated stop as `max_tokens` (ai.md §4.6; Anthropic stop-reasons reference). Both Cores reject partial JSON without a paid corrective retry; stub runner tests assert one request.

### 2026-10-30 — LM Studio working context

- Both Cores trust positive v0 context fields only for `state: loaded`, preferring `loaded_context_length`. Unloaded/unknown contexts use the conservative 4,096-token fallback from ai.md §§6.6/11.5, never a JIT model's advertised maximum. Stub tests cover states and positive loaded lengths.

### 2026-10-30 — lead — ElevenLabs error detail and residency keys

- `ElevenLabsTranscriber` keeps its region. `MapError(status, body, key, region)` reads the object form of `detail` (`{type, code, message, status}`) as well as a string or a list. A 401, `authentication_error` or `invalid_api_key` is a key problem at any status. `TestKeyAsync` keeps its status-only, no-echo mapping (transcription §8.5) and adds `ElevenLabsRegions.ResidencyHint()` to a residency 400 or 401.
- On EU, India or Singapore residency, a key error or a 400 says the region needs a key from a data-residency workspace and points to Region: Default, matching the Mac copy (apps/macos/CONTRACTS.md, same date). The reason is shortened so the hint stays whole inside 200 characters. The Settings Region explanation is owed to the redesigned Settings window.
- Tests: `ElevenLabsTests` covers the object detail, residency key errors, the residency 401, a 300-character reason and the residency key Test (ElevenLabsTests 27/27 and ElevenLabsKeyTestTests 17/17 on the Mac host).

### 2026-10-29 - sol-win-redesign r14 - Native bounds coordinates

- The in-process peer bounds are translated from client to screen coordinates before the right-column checks; native peer identity deduplicates recursive enumeration. The stage container alone owns `PreviewStage`, so a zero-sized missing-video image cannot alias a required stage peer. Required coverage and both-axis containment remain fail closed.

### 2026-10-29 - sol-win-redesign r14 A5 - Keyboard and accessibility

- Word arrows move from `SelectionCaret`, including Shift extension in either direction; the first arrow selects word zero. Keyboard movement announces the caret and selection through the selection notification activity and reveals the word with line margin. The transcript's read-only ValuePattern supplies current selection on refocus without changing its stable accessible Name; Page Up/Down scroll without moving selection.
- Fine tune rebuilds only for a different style/read-only control shape; value, aspect, Undo/Redo, Reset and Busy changes synchronize existing controls in place. Onboarding preserves the focused step choice. Self-removing actions hand focus to a staying sidebar row or Home tile before cancellation; Keep all focuses its first kept clip. Progress controls have explicit names. Spoken Narrator acceptance remains a lead-owned manual gate.

### 2026-10-29 - sol-win-redesign r14 A4 - Viewer bar

- Replaces r12's transport rule: Back and Forward remain 32 by 32 subtle buttons, Play is a round 40 by 40 default neutral button, then AI modified and the time trail as one right-aligned group. The measured row moves that group together below the controls with a 4 epx gap only when necessary; it never shrinks targets or truncates the time.
- Every native fit sample records the transport geometry and checks `ColumnBoundsGate.TransportFailures`: sizes, containment, button and tag/time order, trailing time and inline/stacked placement. Pure negative cases reject the previous undersized and tag-first layouts.

### 2026-10-29 - sol-win-redesign r14 A3 - High Contrast transcript and stage

- Transcript RichTextBlocks disable the automatic contrast backplate; the existing system-colour runs and Composition fills remain visible. Selection uses Highlight/HighlightText; playing uses WindowText/Window, as design sections 4.1 and 5.4.9 require. Loaded and the next render after an effects change refresh the copied overlay brushes.
- Stage messages gain a Window plate and WindowText edge only in High Contrast. The native transcript pixel gate now checks live system fills and glyph colours instead of skipping contrast mode; normal-theme alignment assertions are unchanged. Live desktop verification remains a separate gate.

### 2026-10-29 - sol-win-redesign r14 A1 - Model download confirmation

- `ModelDownloadCopy` supplies the catalogue publisher, attribution, source-page link, dependency-inclusive size and progress for Windows and CLI downloads. Settings confirms with a ContentDialog; the tour's checked licence confirms only the exact model and terms displayed. `models install <id>` is command consent and prints those terms to stderr unless `--quiet`.
- `ModelStore.InstallAsync` without a confirmation callback throws `InvalidOperationException` before any HTTP request; declined confirmation still cancels. Listing, verification and removal need no confirmation. Installs for the same data root and model continue to share the existing in-flight operation.

- **2026-10-28 - sol-win-redesign r12 D1:** Right-column content uses its actual 300-560 epx width, with 16 epx side padding. Frame measures the label and the four complete segments: it is inline iff their natural widths plus 12 epx fit; otherwise Frame sits above the full-width selector with a 12 epx gap. Transport uses the same measurement with a 4 epx gap, moving AI modified above the unchanged controls/time when needed; Windows shows no playback-rate control. Long timecodes are not truncated. Caption summary text wraps beside its chip, with Fine tune below the summary; the stage yields height on the smallest viewer-visible window. Native UIA bounds, untrimmed Frame labels, narrow states and cropped captures are prepared but remain owed until the same VM returns.
- Round 13 hardens that owed gate: named per-state stage/transport/time/Frame/Captions/gallery/Fine tune/Rewrite peers must be realized and nonempty, every onscreen peer fits both column and client axes, and only vertically scrolled peers within an owned, contained ScrollViewer (including the Captions gallery and Rewrite) may be classified scrolled out; each state records required bounds and all unmeasurable role/name evidence, with portable negative and scrolling tests.

### 2026-10-28 — sol-win-redesign round 10 — Transcript geometry lifecycle

- Measure paragraph geometry before changing Run foregrounds or clearing TextHighlighters: either mutation invalidates native text layout. The old ordering cached empty pre-layout rectangles, so the attached, correctly sized Composition layers contained only a gutter rule, not strikes or playing fills. Empty/non-finite measurements are retried after layout; usable rectangles are retained until text, size or zoom invalidates them. Rewrite's diff uses the same lifecycle. No text-control replacement or rendering dependency is introduced.
- ARM64 diagnostics at native scale 1 show the original strike assertion progressing from failure (zero behind shapes, one above shape) to success (one behind, 17 above) after measuring before the style mutation. Subsequent glyph/alignment gates are tracked separately; this is not full native acceptance. Evidence: /tmp/voxclip-win-native-r10/evidence/win-arm64/before and before-style. Portable EmptyPrelayoutBoundsAreRetriedAndValidGeometryIsRetained covers cache retry, retention, invalidation and intentionally hidden runs.

### 2026-10-27 — sol-vl-media-win — Paid WAV durability and source coverage (rounds 2–4)

- `VoiceLabAssetStore.Write(ReadOnlySpan<byte> pcm16, int sampleRate, string projectId, string assetId, string paidRoot, DateTimeOffset createdAt, CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)` and `WriteDerived(ReadOnlySpan<float> samples, ...)` publish canonical mono PCM16 WAVs named only by Core's `VoiceLabAssetNaming.File` (§10.1, §10.4). Derived samples use binary64 multiplication by 32767, ties to even and a ±32767 clamp; nonfinite values are refused before any file is created. Bounds remain 8–192 kHz, at least one frame and at most (UInt32.MaxValue − 36) / 2 frames.
- Publication uses an exclusive same-directory temporary file, WriteThrough and Flush(true), then Windows `MoveFileExW` with only `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, never `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`. Win32 ERROR_FILE_EXISTS and ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS map to AlreadyExists. Portable Mac/Linux verification uses exclusive rename primitives. A successful move establishes publication before readback: length/hash failures report Altered or PublishedUnverified and retain the final file. Native regressions check handle identity at the final name, a racing destination, and failed confirmation. This verifies the selected API and file outcomes, not an actual power-loss experiment.
- A paid-root alias is allowed on the root or any ancestor, as on Mac. Windows follows it when opening the root, pins that directory against deletion with GENERIC_READ, and resolves it once with GetFinalPathNameByHandle. Project, temporary and final handle paths must be contained in that canonical root. Project handles are opened no-follow and their attributes reject reparse points, including a junction targeting another directory inside the root. Only a missing paid-root leaf is created, never its missing data parent. Direct-root and ancestor-junction native cases succeed; below-root junctions are refused. Decisions 2 and 3 resolve both round-one durability/root-alias questions.
- Failed pre-publication cleanup catches IOException and UnauthorizedAccessException without replacing the primary typed failure or cancellation. A typed failure exposes `OrphanedTemporaryPath`; every primary exception, including cancellation, also exposes that path in `Data["OrphanedTemporaryPath"]`. No cleanup deletes or replaces a published final. Four native cases deny deletion with a sharing handle or read-only attribute, each during an I/O failure and cancellation, then remove their own orphan after asserting the original result and preserved paid bytes.
- `VoiceLabSourceAudio.ConversionInputAsync` decodes the requested half-open source-grid range at its native rate. The arithmetic channel mean is computed in binary64, converted to Float32, then written with the same PCM16 rule. Rate, 20 ms minimum, 290 s maximum and exclusive 50,000,000-byte WAV limits are checked before native loading. Continuity uses `tickSamples = ((double)rate * time_base.num) / time_base.den`, then `tolerance = max(1, tickSamples)`, without rounding the tick. All operations are binary64 in that order. The precise sample position is `((best_effort_timestamp * ((double)time_base.num / time_base.den)) − originSeconds) * rate`, with originSeconds from the format start time divided by AV_TIME_BASE, or zero when absent. A frame is contiguous iff `abs(preciseSamplePosition − previousEnd) < tolerance`, strictly; it is placed at previousEnd and advances that end only by its decoded sample count. Otherwise ordinary half-up placement applies and missing requested coverage reports RangeOutsideMedia.
- The bound is strictly one container tick, with a one-sample minimum: both ticks bracketing a continuous position pass, including nearest-rounding and truncating muxers. Repeated real gaps cannot reset the decoded position; once their accumulated timestamp drift reaches the bound, continuity fails. Gaps smaller than one container tick are undetectable; sample-accurate time bases (`tickSamples <= 1`) retain the round-two strictly sub-sample rule. Portable regressions pin both sides of the 48-sample boundary, ±24 samples, 1/90000 and sample-rate time bases, fractional ticks without ceiling, and repeated 40-sample gaps failing at the third packet. Native regressions check every returned sample of the unchanged shared seven-second MKV and a generated continuous MKV, refuse a generated 96-sample MKV gap and a one-sample gap in a fragmented PCM MP4 with time_base 1/48000, and assert the generated stream timestamps. Mac adoption of this same formula is a lead-queued parity follow-up, not this track's work; the prior coarse-container continuity question is resolved by this rule.
- The initial extraction anchor is explicitly the DecodeSession seek anchor: for the same seek, its first timestamped frame starts at `Native.Round(DecodeSession.Timestamp * rate)`, including the container's own first packet. Extraction must not independently realign this frame to a sequential sample grid. This invariant matches preview, export and VoiceLabRenderSession. Positions are exact for sample-accurate time bases (`tickSamples <= 1`, including the MP4/MOV/WAV inputs read by Mac). Coarse Matroska/WebM/FLV positions are timestamp-anchored and differ from sequential decoding by less than one container tick; sub-tick gaps remain undetectable. This is a documented container limit, not a refusal, and its sub-millisecond effect is far below seam crossfades. The unchanged committed MKV is pinned at extraction start 96000 (seek target 1.5 s): its first post-seek audio packet has PTS 1003 ms, half-up anchor 48144 versus sequential frame 48128, an offset of +16 samples (one third ms) within its 48-sample tick. A native every-sample regression compares [96000, 144017) against DecodeSession's timestamp-anchored PCM. A separate same-anchor invariant compares initial placement and every returned sample against sequential PCM shifted by the measured offset. Both assert the immutable fixture hash. The lead will add the matching spec sentence at merge.
- Extraction inspects the raw `best_effort_timestamp` before timestamp or PCM conversion. `AV_NOPTS_VALUE` before any anchor throws typed `VoiceLabSourceAudioErrorCode.Unreadable`, matching Mac's unreadable-input error. After an anchor exists, a missing timestamp places the frame at previousEnd and continues by decoded sample count without converting the sentinel. Portable tests pin both cases at zero, nonzero and nonfinite origins; timestamped-frame cases preserve the DecodeSession origin subtraction and half-up anchor. This check is scoped to source-range extraction: DecodeSession, Native.Seconds and their other consumers retain their existing zero-substitution behavior.
- `DecodeAsset` still snapshots a regular, nonlinked WAV through one checked descriptor, optionally verifies its SHA-256, and downmixes/resamples with swresample to 8–192 kHz. Cancellation and failures remove only the private snapshot. The pinned Windows runtime explicitly refuses FLAC; only stored assets have the eight-channel cap. Very short assets retain their original duration while draining a bounded resampler tail. `Envelope` delegates to Core's mono AudioEnvelopeBuilder. No conform, loudness, renderer, provider, or paid-network behavior changes.
- Both calls in `WrittenV3AssetPreparesAndOneChangedByteIsAltered` now use the merged internal scratch-root PrepareAsync overload under the test's own temporary root. The test checks snapshot placement, cleanup after an altered-file refusal, immutable prepared bytes, and cleanup on handle disposal. The upstream merge preserves the capacity/render scratch threading and concurrent ledger entries.
- Round-two verification: local Release cross-platform build has zero warnings/errors; Core DLL reports 1,899 total, 1,871 passed, 28 skipped; portable Media reports 516 total, 511 passed, 5 skipped, no failures. New native cases write a named PASS record only after their assertions and private-storage cleanup to `voice-lab-media-r2-tests.log` beside the existing CI E2E evidence folder. File-system-only cases carry RequiresWindows; decoder cases also carry RequiresFFmpeg. The private VM did not answer its bounded SSH probe; native verification uses the permitted branch-only CI dispatch instead. No live/provider calls or fixture-generator changes.
- Round-three local verification: Release cross-platform build has zero warnings/errors; Core DLL reports 1,899 total, 1,871 passed, 28 skipped; portable Media reports 532 total, 527 passed, 5 skipped, no failures. The three portable continuity tests contribute 22 passing cases. All four native continuity tests use the same named-PASS evidence helper after cleanup. The bounded VM SSH probe timed out again; native verification uses branch-only CI. Local FFmpeg/ffprobe generation probes separately confirm all ten packet timestamps for the continuous MKV, 96-sample MKV gap and fragmented one-sample MP4 gap; these probes are not substitutes for native extraction tests.

### 2026-10-27 — sol-vl-word-diff round 2: current API and verification

This entry supersedes the historical round-1 API and figures below. Decisions 1–8 are implemented in order, one commit each; `origin/main` `5d6a295a5b0c4a9c63701a3f72255cd487d1dcc6` is merged, with all entries from both contract histories retained and track entries first.

- **Whole timed items (§8.2 steps 3–6).** Raw `ops` remain token-level, but actionable hunks widen to entire source items before classification, retain kept surface tokens positionally, and coalesce on shared items or touching regions. Partial deletion/replacement is `voice`, not an item cut. New hunk field `CorrectedTexts: IReadOnlyList<string>` is empty except for `textOnly`, where it contains one complete corrected text per `SourceWordIds`, positionally joining each item's new tokens with single spaces. Source indices still address word-only tokens, now at whole-item boundaries. Insert-only anchors are unchanged. The two-equal-word merge threshold counts distinct source word items, not tokens. Six shared whole-item/bridge regressions cover partial cut, partial replace, interior insertion, partial case correction, two edits in one item, and a three-token bridge item.
- **Nonwords (§8.2 step 6; §8.4 step 4).** Retain events and unlabeled speech in source order as voice-merge barriers, regardless of count or time; insert-only hunks use their anchor position. New hunk field `AbsorbedItemIds: IReadOnlyList<string>` lists strictly interior nonword IDs in source order when a single user-edit voice hunk straddles them. Accepting that hunk replaces those items; the App must show each as removed in the read-only diff line. Cut/textOnly hunks affect only word IDs and have empty absorbed IDs. Ten shared vectors cover both barrier kinds, insertion anchors, one equal word, a 0.1 s gap, both absorption kinds, cut/correction preservation, and exclusion of edge nonwords. No App or design.md edit is made here; the lead adds the matching design sentence at merge.
- **Header pricing (§8.2; §8.4 step 4).** `VoiceLabDiffSummary.Make(..., IReadOnlyList<string?>? patchTexts = null)`. Supply one optional complete patch text per voice hunk in order, including left window bridge + new words + right window bridge. A missing entry or omitted list returns nil/null cost and omits the amount suffix; never price hunk tokens as fallback. A supplied count different from the voice-hunk count throws `VoiceLabLengthBudgetException` with `VoiceLabLengthBudgetErrorCode.InvalidInput`. Only a fully populated list can price its total Unicode scalars, with no separator between hunks. Shared vectors distinguish 3-scalar hunk `new` from 14-scalar complete `left new right`, all-missing text, and one missing text among two windows; the existing focused test pins mismatched-count refusal.
- **Fit quote (§8.9.1).** `VoiceLabFitMeter.Measure(..., double rate, string newWords, bool multipleHunks = false)`. `newWords` is required even for one hunk: the hunk's `NewTokens` joined with single spaces (`string.Join(' ', hunk.NewTokens)`). They include the diff-merge bridge words a merged hunk re-voices (§8.2 step 6) and never the window-search bridges (§8.4 step 4). Duration still uses complete patch `text`; multi-hunk quotes always use `newWords`, never that complete text. `multi-hunk-quote-excludes-bridges` and `multi-hunk-quote-includes-merge-bridges` pin the distinction. Candidate provenance/key/seconds/fallback are documented in the following round-2 entry.
- **Current shared fixtures.** `word-diff-cases.json`: **129 cases, 261,536 bytes**, SHA-256 `cacfa995d303667bb4b84ac7ffba38378769d8cc2a05a639c7c90f25a00e6972`. `length-budget-cases.json`: **53 cases, 46,301 bytes**, SHA-256 `8819ee96ec3501cc192d3325c52d5b811a5ac5a8188230dc3dfc516d8befe371`. The lead's merge added `multi-hunk-quote-includes-merge-bridges` and made `anchor-after-event` a true insert-only hunk anchored after an event (anchor 0.5 s); the former replace case is now `replace-before-event`. Only the three token-limit vectors use declarative repetitions: `sourceRepeat` (`token`, `count`, `idPrefix`, `timingStep`; item `i` is `idPrefix + i` over [`i × timingStep`, start + `timingStep / 2`]), `newTextRepeat` (`count` copies of `token` joined by single spaces) and `expected.repeatedOps` (ordered op kind, source/new tokens, ID prefix and count), as shared/fixtures/voice-lab/README.md specifies. Each suite independently expands source IDs/times and separately specified expected ops, checking all 2,000 ordered IDs/tokens, empty hunks, budget, summary, and exact 2,001-side error/side/count/limit; no production diff constructs expectations. All other vectors remain literal. Both touched stdlib generators reproduce byte-identical output twice, all 15 Voice Lab generators run without tracked drift, and `check_drift.py` passes.
- **Merged-tree verification.** `nice swift build` passes; full Core reports **596 tests in 47 suites**, eight expected skip notices, no failures, replaying all 180 shared vectors. The required 300 ms slow-expression test build passes with no track-added expression flagged; only unchanged App `DebugTranscriptPerformance.swift:22` warns (483 ms). Local Xcode 27 is not hosted Xcode 26 evidence. New widening/repetition arithmetic intermediates and conversion closure parameters have explicit types; no Sendable callback or noncopyable capture is added. Cross-platform .NET Release builds with **0 warnings, 0 errors**. The direct serial Core runner reports **2,081 total, 2,053 passed, 28 skipped, 0 failures**; the focused class passes **182/182**. Shared/schema paths are absolute, schema validation required, live/download switches off, and Windows data isolated at a literal temporary path. Builds run one platform at a time under `nice`, with at least 84 GiB free on Data.
- **Hygiene and handoff.** Exact workflow secret pattern over tracked files (including every changed file), PowerShell ASCII, env/signing exclusions, 357 strict JSON files, editing/LLM/token regeneration, 16 design tests, 19 caption styles, six schema inputs, version agreement, 14 release-tool tests, launch-media budget, eight offline media tests, four static-page tests, and whitespace checks pass. ### 2026-10-27 — sol-vl-word-diff round 1: historical Rewrite consumer API

All additions are pure in `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/VoiceLab/`, owned by `sol-vl-word-diff`, with identical Mac type/field names under platform casing. No App, persistence, dependencies or provider calls change.

- **`VoiceLabWordDiff.cs`.** `VoiceLabWordDiff.Diff(IReadOnlyList<TimedWord> source, string newText) -> VoiceLabWordDiffResult`. Pass selected items. Only `Word` items participate, through existing whitespace/NFC/matching helpers. IDs must be nonempty/unique, word text nonblank, starts ordered/nonnegative, and times finite with `End >= Start`. `VoiceLabWordDiffException.Code` (`VoiceLabWordDiffErrorCode`) is `InvalidInput` or `TokenLimit`; the latter includes `Side: VoiceLabDiffSide?` (`Source | NewText`), `Count: int?`, `Limit=2000`. The 2,000-token per-side guard precedes DP allocation. InvalidInput has null side/count.
- **Diff output.** `VoiceLabWordDiffResult(Ops: IReadOnlyList<VoiceLabDiffOp>, Hunks: IReadOnlyList<VoiceLabDiffHunk>)`. Op fields: `Kind: VoiceLabDiffOpKind` (`Equal | Delete | Insert`), `SourceWordId`, `SourceToken`, `NewToken` (`string?`); inserts lack source fields, deletes lack new tokens. Hunk fields: `Kind: VoiceLabDiffHunkKind` (`Cut | TextOnly | Voice`), `SourceWordIds`, `NewTokens`, `BridgeWordIds` (`IReadOnlyList<string>`), `SourceStartIndex`, `SourceEndIndex` (`int`), `AnchorSeconds: double?`. IDs are distinct in source order. Half-open indices address word-only **tokens**, not transcript items. Inserts anchor at next token start, final token end, or 0. §8.2 pins suffix-DP forward delete-on-tie backtracking, stable deletes-before-inserts per replace region, and merge across equal ops at <=2 words OR end-to-start time <=0.35 s. Bridges (corrected surfaces included) are spoken; absorbed TextOnly hunks are not duplicated.
- **`VoiceLabPatchBudget.cs`.** `VoiceLabPatchBudget.Evaluate(VoiceLabWordDiffResult diff, IReadOnlyList<TimedWord> transcript) -> VoiceLabPatchBudgetResult(VoiceTokenCount: int, WordItems: int, Flag: VoiceLabPatchBudgetFlag, Copy: string?)`. Pass the **whole transcript**. Count merged voice-hunk new tokens including bridges; only Word items enlarge the denominator. `ReRecordInstead` iff `count * 100 > 15 * wordItems || count > 60` using 64-bit products, otherwise `Allowed`. `DisabledCopy` is exactly "That's a lot to re-voice. Re-record these sentences instead."; allowed copy is null. Cuts/corrections remain enabled.
- **`VoiceLabLengthBudget.cs`: rate.** `VoiceLabLengthBudget.Rate(IReadOnlyList<TimedWord> transcript, double pauseThresholdSeconds = 1, IReadOnlyList<VoiceLabRateCandidate>? candidates = null) -> VoiceLabSpeechRate`. Supply the take's threshold and stored candidates for **one** selected (voice, model, language) key. Inputs: `VoiceLabRateCandidate(string Text, double FirstCharStart, double LastCharEnd)`. Nonempty candidates override transcript evidence; otherwise existing pause detection on all items and nonword barriers define voiced word spans. Result fields: `CharactersPerSecond: double`, `Source: VoiceLabSpeechRateSource` (`Transcript | Candidates`), `TextLength: int`, `VoicedSeconds: double`. Length is NFC letters/digits. `VoiceLabLengthBudgetException.Code` (`VoiceLabLengthBudgetErrorCode`) is `InvalidInput` for malformed inputs/overflow or `RateUnavailable` for no positive evidence; bad candidates never silently fall back.
- **Length ranges.** `Estimate(string text, double rate) -> double`; `Calculate(VoiceLabWindow window, double? incomingSilence, double? outgoingSilence, int bridgeLength, double rate) -> VoiceLabLengthRange`; `Passage(VoiceLabWindow window, IReadOnlyList<TimedWord> transcript, double? incomingSilence, double? outgoingSilence, double rate) -> VoiceLabLengthRange`; `FullTake(IReadOnlyList<TimedWord> transcript, double rate) -> VoiceLabLengthRange`. Fields: `TargetSeconds`, `EdgePadSeconds` (`double`), `BridgeLength`, `MinChars`, `MaxChars` (`int`), `Advisory: bool`. Feed `VoiceLabWindowPlanner.LendableSilence`'s pair directly: hard=null, gap/none=finite nonnegative. Place the **whole selection as one replace hunk** for Passage, also for each Fix-with-AI line; it resolves bridge IDs through VoiceLabTextLength. FullTake is advisory, first-word start through last-word end, `e=f=0`. §8.9.1 fixes binary64 order and default-trim edge padding. Results fit int; rewrite services retain 600 s / 5,000-character request validation, not calculator clamping.
- **`VoiceLabFitMeter.cs`.** `VoiceLabFitMeter.Measure(string text, VoiceLabWindow window, double? incomingSilence, double? outgoingSilence, double rate, bool multipleHunks = false, string? newWords = null) -> VoiceLabFitMeterResult`. Text is the **complete patch**, window-search and diff-merge bridges included. Fields: `EstimateSeconds`, `GapSeconds (=w)`, `LowerBoundSeconds`, `UpperBoundSeconds` (`double`), `Verdict: VoiceLabFitVerdict` (`Fits | WillChangeLength`), `Copy: string`. Inclusive lower bound subtracts hard=0.06 or gap/none=0.3 pads; upper borrows `max(0, outsideSilence-0.15)` only at gap seams. None seams never borrow. Copy uses EditingFormat.ToFixed1; multiple hunks add `“new words” · ` from optional `newWords` (default: `text`), allowing window-search bridges to stay out of the quote. §8.9.1 explicitly reconciles borrowing with §8.9.2; no paid-take planner change.
- **`VoiceLabDiffSummary.cs`.** `VoiceLabDiffSummary.Make(VoiceLabWordDiffResult diff, IReadOnlyList<VoiceLabFitMeterResult> meters, VoiceLabPriceCatalog? catalog = null, string? model = null, int candidates = 1, double? characterMultiplier = null, IReadOnlyList<string>? patchTexts = null) -> VoiceLabDiffSummaryResult`. Supply one meter per merged voice hunk, in order. Fields: `CutCount`, `RevoicedPhrases` (`int`), `EstimateSeconds: double`, `CostEstimate: CostEstimate?`, `Copy: string`. Cuts count distinct cut IDs; duration sums each hunk once, not each candidate. Optional `patchTexts` supplies one complete spoken patch per voice hunk, including window-search bridges; otherwise use each hunk's space-joined new tokens. Price Unicode scalars without an inter-hunk separator, not letters/digits. A mismatched patch-text count throws InvalidInput. Existing VoiceLabPricing/VoiceLabCostPresentation determines amount. No catalog/no voices yields null cost; unknown model yields unknown-price cost and no amount. Singular/plural copy is pure, with no approval/spend implied.

These five files and all result/error/enum declarations named above extend the public declaration inventory. The round-1 verification entry records vector counts, hashes and tests.

### 2026-10-27 — sol-win-redesign round 3 — A1 pending edits

Design §13 has no save-failure action string; open copy question: "Could not save this project. It is still open with your edits. Use Save project as… or check the folder, then try again."

### 2026-10-27 — Windows early Ctrl+C audit (sol-followups-2)

- Lead's narrowed item 3 is audit-only: no Windows CLI product change, timing hook, new test or architecture wording change is needed. `Program.cs` cancels the workflow token on `Console.CancelKeyPress`; `CliApplication.Stub` checks it before the export command runs. `RunAsync` maps `OperationCanceledException` to exit 6 with an empty message. Human cancellation is silent; JSON retains its existing `cancelled` envelope.

| Pre-export step | Token observation already present |
| --- | --- |
| Share-text preflight / runtime agreement | Synchronous launch checks; the command-entry `ThrowIfCancellationRequested` follows them, before source/output work. |
| Project/source load | `OpenExportInputAsync` passes `ct` to `ProjectStore.LoadAsync`; atomic file I/O and source resolution observe it. Raw/model decoding is synchronous between those boundaries. |
| Media probe | `ProbeInputAsync` passes `ct` to `FfmpegMediaProbe.ProbeAsync`; `MediaThread.Run` checks it before native work. An in-flight native call must return. |
| Transcript read/decode | `ReadTranscriptAsync` passes `ct` to file I/O; JSON decode and word validation are synchronous, followed by token-aware caption/export preparation. |
| Content key / transcription preparation | `ComputeContentKeyAsync` checks before work, during reads and after hashing; extraction, model lookup and transcription receive `ct`, and cancelled extraction cleans its temporary file. |
| Edit application / output plan | Synchronous pure Core work; the next caption-build or exporter-entry boundary observes `ct`. |
| Voice Lab preflight / capture | Synchronous Core validation and immutable capture; no scratch is created here. Media Voice Lab preparation receives `ct` after the export-entry guard. |
| Captions | `CaptionEngine.BuildAsync` receives `ct`; style/resource setup before it is synchronous. |
| Exporter setup / capacity validation | Synchronous snapshot, destinations, `Exporter.Validate` and capacity checks; `PrepareAsync` checks `ct` before creating partials or Voice Lab scratch. |
| Audio / SRT sidecars | `WriteWaveAsync` and `WriteAsync` receive `ct`; created sidecar partials are owned and deleted in `ExportAsync`'s `finally`. |
| Export / publication | The exporter guards entry before any partial; publication has an explicit cancellation checkpoint plus token-aware rollback. |

- Existing real-command regression `CliOutputParityTests.CancelledExportJsonHasAnEmptyMessage` supplies a pre-cancelled token and requires exit 6 and an empty cancellation message before even opening its missing input. No export, partial or scratch work is reached. `CancellationBetweenPublicationRenamesRestoresAllDestinations` covers rollback after publication begins. No genuinely uncovered preparation step or product defect was found, so no redundant test was added and no tight timing assertion was introduced.

Both strict parsers reject number tokens over 1,024 bytes (ai §4.5, §6.8, §12.8). The 35 shared CLI vectors cover those rules and Codex exec/app-server LF-only splitting with CRLF, trailing CR, U+2028 and BOM.

### 2026-10-27 — Voice Lab fit planner (opus-vl-fit-planner)

- Added the §8.9.2 silence-only duration fit to Core, with the Mac Core's names, in `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/VoiceLab/VoiceLabFitPlanner.cs`: `VoiceLabFitPlanner.Plan(VoiceLabWindow window, double? incomingSilence, double? outgoingSilence, VoiceLabFitTake take, IReadOnlyList<string> tokens, double sourceSampleRate)`.
  - It returns `VoiceLabFitResult.Exact(Plan)` or `VoiceLabFitResult.ChangedDuration(Plan, NaturalOut, ResidualSeconds)`.
  - `VoiceLabFitPlan` holds `Edits` (`VoiceLabFitEdit(Kind, Start, Count)` in asset frames, with `VoiceLabFitEditKind.Remove` or `Insert`), `Incoming` and `Outgoing` (`VoiceLabFitSeam(PadFrames, BorrowedSamples)`), `TrimStartFrame`, `TrimEndFrame`, `WindowStart`, `WindowEnd`, `WindowStartSample`, `WindowEndSample`, `OutputFrames` and `Words`.
  - `VoiceLabFitTake(AssetFrames, AssetSampleRate, TrimStart, TrimEnd, Words, Envelope)` carries the take.
  - Punctuation is judged by `Rune.GetUnicodeCategory` on the token's last scalar.
  - A `Gap` seam's silence is its lendable silence, which the new public `VoiceLabWindowPlanner.LendableSilence(Project, VoiceLabOutputScope, VoiceLabWindow)` returns as `(double? Incoming, double? Outgoing)` in seconds, null for a `Hard` seam, and transcript silence to the nearest window word for `None`. It is the kept source contiguous with the seam on its outer side, as §8.4 step 5 measures it for the final window, up to the nearest kept item outside the window: it stops at a kept item, a cut item's reach, a cut pause, a clip edge or another replacement's outer fade half. `Place` and the helper share the private `Take` and `ScopeSource` types, which hold `Place`'s own take checks and §8.4 scope setup, so `Place`'s results and error order are unchanged. The internal `VoiceLabGeometry.Check` takes a nullable envelope.

  No caller uses the planner yet.
- `VoiceLabPatchConstants` gains `SEAM_PAD_MAX`, `HARD_SEAM_PAD_MAX`, `PAUSE_FLOOR_PHRASE`, `PAUSE_FLOOR_PUNCT`, `PAUSE_GROWTH_MAX`, `LENGTH_AIM_MIN` and `LENGTH_AIM_MAX` (§8.3), as on Mac.
- `VoiceLabGeometryErrorCode` gains `FitTouchesVoice` after `RoomToneUnavailable`, so no existing numeric value moves. The planner checks every plan against the never-touch-voice invariant and throws it on failure; it is a planner fault. The validation order, the refusal codes, the 2^31-frame asset limit, the 1 Hz to 1 MHz source rate and the trim order match the Mac entry. Review round 2 changed the planner as on Mac: a negative rounding residual that no pad or trim can take raises the borrow to the fewest source samples whose widened window holds the planned frames, and pads the gap seam that borrowed, so it is never refused with `InvalidWordTiming`; and each word's times are clamped to `WindowEnd − WindowStart`, plus `ResidualSeconds` after a partial fit. ### 2026-10-27 — Caption gallery review fixes (round 2)

- Supersedes the round-1 override, lookup and failure-backdrop wording below. Implements captions §6.1, §8.1–8.2, §15.2–15.3 and design §5.4.2–5.4.4, §6.1 on merged `origin/main` `196b25c7`. ### 2026-10-27 — Voice Lab pricing, cost presentation and spend ledger (opus-vl-pricing)

- The lead's decisions for voice-lab §3.1 and §3.2, with a typed hook for §3.3 live prices, revised after review rounds 1 to 3 and matching the Mac Core entry of the same date. This is an in-place Core change. The approval flow, quote fingerprints and request bodies are unchanged. `ElevenLabsVoiceLab` records each accepted action itself. App and CLI need no wiring: neither constructs the service, and no App or CLI path calls a Voice Lab provider outside it. The CLI's `HttpClient` serves model downloads and LLM connectors. Old → new:
  - The public `ElevenLabsVoiceLab` constructor gains `VoiceLabSpendLedger? spend = null`. Null there means `<data>/VoiceLab/spend.json` from `DataLocations.Resolve()`. The internal `ForTesting(...)` gains the same parameter, where null records nothing.
  - `VoiceLabReceipt` gains a final positional `VoiceLabSpendWarning? SpendWarning`, and `VoiceLabAudioResult.SpendWarning` and `VoiceLabCloneResult.SpendWarning` forward it. Deletion returns the receipt itself.
  - A lip sync of zero seconds is invalid input to `VoiceLabPricing.Estimate`, and `Kind` confirms every lip sync, even at $0.
  - The private `SendAsync` takes the approved action as `billing` and records it on the reply's 2xx status line (round 4). The round-3 `SpentAsync` is gone.
  - `ElevenLabsVoiceLab(IKeyProvider, ElevenLabsRegion, ILog?)` and the internal `ForTesting(...)` gain `VoiceLabPriceCatalog? prices = null`. Null loads `catalog/voice-lab.json` through `VoiceLabPrices.Load()` on first use; a missing or defective catalog is `SharedResourcesMissing`.
  - The private `Unknown(operation, units, unit)` is replaced by the public `CostEstimate Estimate(VoiceLabOperation operation, string? modelId, double units, int candidates = 1)`, which uses the discovered credit multiplier.
  - Quotes carry catalog estimates. TTS counts runes with the unit `character`. The voice changer counts `wav.Seconds` with the unit `second` (was minutes, `minute`). Creating or deleting a voice is one free `call`.
  - Discovery refuses a multiplier outside 0.01–100 (`MalformedResponse`).
  - New `VoiceLabEstimate.ToCostEstimate()`.
- New public API; every type is listed in the declaration inventory under opus-vl-pricing:
  - `VoiceLabPricedOperation(Provider, Operation, Endpoint, Unit)` with `ElevenLabsSpeechToText`, `ElevenLabsAudioIsolation`, `ElevenLabsVoiceChange`, `ElevenLabsTextToSpeech`, `ElevenLabsForcedAlignment`, `ElevenLabsCreateClone`, `ElevenLabsDeleteClone`, `SyncsoLipsync` and `FalLipsync(endpoint)`.
  - `VoiceLabPriceFormula` and `VoiceLabPriceEntry`. `VoiceLabPriceCatalog` has `Format`, `Entries`, `Parse(ReadOnlyMemory<byte>)` and `Find(operation, model)`, and `VoiceLabPrices.Load(string? sharedRoot = null)` loads it.
  - `VoiceLabPricing.Estimate(operation, model, units, candidates, characterMultiplier, catalog)`, `Apply(VoiceLabProviderPrice, CostEstimate)` and `IsCharacterMultiplier(double)` (0.01–100). The hook uses `VoiceLabProviderPrice(decimal Usd, int? Credits, VoiceLabProviderPriceBasis Basis)` and `VoiceLabProviderPriceBasis` (`ProviderEstimate`, `ProviderPricingApi`). C# takes every argument of `Estimate` explicitly, because a defaulted parameter cannot come before `catalog`.
  - `VoiceLabCostPresentation` with `Kind(estimate, isLipSync, providerName, VoiceLabMonthlyCap? cap = null)` → `VoiceLabCostPresentationResult(VoiceLabCostPresentationKind Kind, string Text)`, `Format(decimal)`, `ButtonTitle(action, int? takes, kind)` and `CapWarning(cap, providerName)`.
  - `VoiceLabMonthlyCap(decimal Limit, decimal Spent)` with `IsCrossedBy(CostEstimate)`.
  - `VoiceLabSpendTotal(decimal Usd, int UnknownCount)`, and `VoiceLabSpendDocument` with `Format`, `Months`, `Parse(byte[])`, `Month(start, TimeZoneInfo)`, `Total`, `Record` and `Encode()`.
  - `VoiceLabSpendLedger(DataLocations, TimeZoneInfo?, TimeSpan?)` and `(string filePath, TimeZoneInfo?, TimeSpan?)`. It offers `FilePath`, `TimeZone` (null means the system zone), `LockTimeout`, `ReadTimeout`, `Month`, `ReadAsync(ct)` → `VoiceLabSpendDocument?`, `TotalAsync(provider, start, ct)` → `VoiceLabSpendTotal?`, `RecordAsync(estimate, start, ct = default)` and `WouldCrossAsync(cap, provider, estimate, start, ct)`. `RecordAsync`'s token applies only to the wait for the lock.
  - `VoiceLabSpendWarning(VoiceLabSpendWarningKind Kind, string? BackupFileName)` with `ReplacedCorruptLedger`, `LockTimedOut`, `Cancelled`, `WriteFailed` and `InvalidEstimate`. The lead accepted `Cancelled` in round 3.
  - `SpendWarning` on `VoiceLabReceipt`, `VoiceLabAudioResult` and `VoiceLabCloneResult`.
  - `AppSettings.GetVoiceLabMonthlyCap(provider)` and `SetVoiceLabMonthlyCap(provider, decimal?)`.
  - `AtomicFile.WriteAsync` gains an optional `UnixFileMode? unixCreateMode`; existing callers are unchanged.
- Rules are the Mac entry's. On Windows specifically:
  - **Recording:** as soon as a 2xx status arrives, after the redirect check, `SendAsync` starts `RecordAsync` through `Task.Run` with `CancellationToken.None`, never on the caller's synchronization context. It awaits the record in `finally`, so a cancelled, broken or oversized body is counted before its error reaches the caller. The service therefore never gets `Cancelled`. The start time comes from the service's `TimeProvider`. A deletion answered 404 is not a 2xx reply and records nothing.
  - **Locks:** a writer takes an exclusive `FileShare.None` open of `spend.json.lock` for up to 5 s, and a reader a shared `FileShare.Read` open for at most 0.5 s. .NET maps these to `flock` off Windows. Only a sharing or lock violation (HResult 32 or 33), or `EWOULDBLOCK` off Windows, is waited for. Any other `IOException`, such as read-only media or a full disk, is `WriteFailed` at once.
  - **Directory:** only `VoiceLab` itself is created, inside an existing data folder, with mode 0700 off Windows.
  - **Durability:** `AtomicFile` flushes the 0600 temporary file with `Flush(true)` before `File.Replace`.
  - **Time zone:** without an injected zone, `Month` calls `TimeZoneInfo.ClearCachedData()` before reading `TimeZoneInfo.Local`. This is cheap, because only the local zone reloads when next asked for. It is safe, because held instances stay valid. A zone change therefore applies to the next action.
  - **Byte-order mark:** the check is spelled `'\uFEFF'`.
- **Real Windows share-mode locking is unverified.** These suites ran on macOS ARM64, where .NET emulates `FileShare.None` with an advisory `flock`. Windows's real sharing violations, and `File.Replace` racing a reader, wait for the Windows build VM. `VoiceLabPricingTests.WindowsShareModesKeepWritersApartAndReadersOutOfTheReplaceWindow` is that VM test: it skips elsewhere. It checks that a second exclusive open fails with HResult 32, that a writer then times out, and that a reader racing 100 records never sees a total fall. The same race runs on every platform in `ReadersRacingAWriterSeeOnlyWholeLedgers`.
- The fixture is `shared/fixtures/voice-lab/pricing-cases.json` with 247 cases, SHA-256 `5e2adb4f69f52c92933f5e2077712b91710b7fcc6ce3e7848d1b1de975bb62c7`:
  - 117 estimates, 5 provider prices, 29 formats, 21 presentations, 10 buttons and 6 quotes;
  - 47 ledger cases;
  - 12 settings values.

  `VoiceLabPricingTests` replays every case.
- The App and CLI ship `shared\**` except the fixtures, so the catalog ships without a per-file list.
- Verification on macOS ARM64 (round 4), on the tree merged with main `f777c433`:
  - `dotnet build VoxClip.CrossPlatform.slnf -c Release -nodeReuse:false -p:UseSharedCompilation=false` had 0 warnings and 0 errors.
  - Core tests passed 1,509 of 1,537, with 28 skipped (the 27 existing prerequisites and the Windows VM test) and 0 failed.
  - The pricing class ran 27 tests: 26 passed and 1 VM test was skipped. The three service tests are the Mac ones, with a stubbed `HttpMessageHandler`. On Windows the read-only case uses a read-only lock file, because Windows ignores the read-only attribute on folders.
  - The status-line test serves its bodies through a scripted `Stream`. It records once each for a 200 cancelled after a first 64-byte chunk, an `IOException` after that chunk, a `Content-Length` over the 44 MiB limit, an endless body that passes the limit, and a cancellation after the last byte, which still returns the audio.
  - A cancellation check after the read loop, and recording only after the whole body, each failed the status-line test.
  - Round 3: recording before `SendAsync` failed the first service test.
  - Round 2: with the writer's lock opened `FileShare.ReadWrite`, the two-writer test failed in 3 of 3 runs.

### 2026-10-27 — Shared share-text rules and disclosure line in Core (share-core)

- Same API as the Mac Core (publishing.md §2.1, voice-lab.md §11.3). `Domain/Share` declares `ShareDestination` (`X`, `LinkedIn`, `VoxConvo`), the internal `ShareTextRules`, and `ShareDisclosure` (`RemovedWarning`, `ShareAnyway`, `RestoreLine`, `XMadeWithAiTip`, `IsPresent(caption, line)`, and the internal `FieldText(savedOrDefault, line, rules)`). `Services/Share/SharePostText` (`Preflight`, `Limit`, `XWeight`, `LinkedInLength`, `VoxConvoLength`, `ComposerText(caption, destination, disclosureLine)`, `DefaultCaption(title, words)`, `FieldText(savedOrDefault, line)`, `CapCaption(caption)`) loads `shared/data/share-text.json` once through `SharedResources.ResolveRoot()` and passes the rules to Domain. The public field text was `ShareDisclosure.FieldText(savedOrDefault, line)`, which read `ShareTextRulesLoader.Current`: file I/O and global state in Domain, against architecture §3. It is now `SharePostText.FieldText`, as on the Mac. A missing or invalid file throws `VoxClipException` with `SharedResourcesMissing`. A lone surrogate reads as U+FFFD, as a Swift string holds it, so NFC never throws.
- Fails at launch, never mid-session: `AppServices.InitializeAsync` calls `SharePostText.Preflight()` after `ResolveRoot()` and maps a failure to `StartupNotice`; `voxclip-cli` runs it before every command except help, `--version` and `version`. A failed load is kept for the process too, as the Mac keeps its `Result`. `ShareTextRulesLoader.CreateCache` makes a `Lazy` with ExecutionAndPublication, which caches the exception; the earlier PublicationOnly walked the shared root again on every call. `AFailedLoadIsKeptForTheProcess` loads from a root without the file, adds the file, and still gets the first failure after one load. `ResolveRoot()` accepts a root only when it has `caption-styles` and `data/share-text.json` (architecture §2.4). The `ExportDialog` caption counter shows a failure in the dialog's notice; Open X already runs inside `Ui.RunAsync`.
- `FieldText` cuts the caption, never the line, to keep the field within 3,000 scalars (voice-lab.md §11.3). A rule-4 cut never ends inside a grapheme cluster. `IsHost` scans only host characters. The composer bisects the rule-4 candidates, and the rule-5 cuts except for X, each measured whole. X rule 5 is `LastFittingXCut`, the one-pass cut of the Mac ledger, over UTF-16 indices: a surrogate pair weighs on its high surrogate. `ShareTextRules` rejects the same data files as the Mac (`StandsAlone`), which replaces the surrogate check.
- Worst case before: the first word with `.` or `:` was scanned from the longest cut down, each cut measured whole. At 3,000 scalars, in a Release build (a console harness over `SharePostText.ComposerText`), it took 70 to 234 ms with the default tiered JIT: `今日は12:30に…` 234 ms, `VoxClip1.0で…` 189 ms, 1,500 × `a.` 168 ms, `a.` + 2,998 `a` 139 ms, `data:` + 2,995 `A` 70 ms. With tiered compilation off it took 117 to 200 ms. At 10,000 scalars it took 1.02 s, or 1.70 s with tiering off. That ran in `ShareHelpers.OpenComposerAsync` on the UI thread. After: 0.4 to 6.7 ms (first calls, tier 0), 0.3 to 0.8 ms with tiering off, and 1.2 ms at 10,000, with the same outputs. `XFirstWordCutsMatchTheLongestFirstScan` compares the one-pass cut with the scan on 120 random first words (76 cut, 17 keeping a URL).
- Data and fixture as in the Mac ledger: `share-text.json` sha256 `3a620cb39601457e39e7a665e44accece9580d18754267caf6bbab9279b1ead0`; `cases.json` with 76 cases, sha256 `e505f150e903ee0e970c92450a33a81f164a59ae2a42c5af6f0726fd91879522`. .NET `StringInfo` text elements match every fixture cluster. `Release-Common.ps1` ships `data` as a seventh runtime folder through `Get-ReleaseRuntimeFolders`, and `test-release-tools.ps1` checks that list covers every shared folder the C# sources read (not run here: no PowerShell on this Mac). The App and CLI projects already copy all of `shared/` except fixtures.
- `VoiceLabRenderContract` picks the Estonian line when the primary language subtag (`Split('-')[0]`) is `et`, compared with `OrdinalIgnoreCase`; before, any `et` prefix matched.
- App, not compiled on the Mac (hosted CI builds WinUI): `ShareHelpers.OpenComposerAsync` sends `ComposerText(caption, ShareDestination.X, null)` to X and still copies the full caption. It copies and reveals at once and cuts the X text with `Task.Run`; the await resumes on the UI thread's dispatcher, which launches the composer. `ExportDialog.OpenComposerAsync` ignores Open clicks while one is in flight (`composing`). The caption field caps at 3,000 Unicode scalars, as `share.captions` and the Mac do: a `TextChanging` handler cuts typed, pasted and loaded text with `SharePostText.CapCaption` and keeps the caret where it was. `MaxLength` counts UTF-16 units, so an emoji counted twice and the field stopped near 1,500 emoji; it is now only a loose bound of 6,000. That handler and all of `ShareHelpers.cs` type-check on the Mac against the WinUI 2.3.9 and Windows SDK 10.0.26100 reference assemblies in the NuGet cache, with no warnings or errors. The rest of `ExportDialog.cs` needs the App's own types, so hosted CI is the full build. `ExportDialog` shows `X {weight}/280 · LinkedIn {n:N0}/3,000`, in `VcStatusWarningBrush` over 280. `AppServices` and `ExportDialog` changed for the preflight as above.
- CLI: `CliSharedResourcesTests` runs the built `voxclip-cli.dll` as a child process, because a process keeps its first load. It has no category traits, so it runs on every OS. Terms are accepted as in `MediaCliE2ETests`, and `VOXCLIP_SHARED_DIR` points at a copy of `caption-styles` and `data` in the run's own temporary directory (`shared`, beside the data directory), so overlapping runs never touch each other's copy. With the copy intact, `probe` of an absent file exits 3 with `mediaMissing`. With `share-text.json` missing, and then containing `{`, it exits 3 with `sharedResourcesMissing`, while `--help`, `--version` and `version` exit 0.
- Verification on macOS: the cross-platform Release build (`--no-incremental`) had zero warnings and errors. After merging main at `217cd334`, Core tests ran 1,489: 1,462 passed, 27 existing skips, no failures (10 in `SharePostTextTests`). `CliTests` and `CliSharedResourcesTests` passed 35 with 1 FFmpeg skip.

### 2026-10-27 — Voice Lab patch window geometry (wave2-vl-seams)

- Added pure Core geometry for a re-voiced passage in `Domain/VoiceLab`, with the same names as the Mac Core. `VoiceLabPatchConstants` holds the §8.3 constants this geometry uses, `VOICED_FRAME_LEVEL` and `RMS_FRAME`. `VoiceLabWindowPlanner.Place(project, scope, envelope, firstWordId, lastWordId)` places a replace window for a `Project` and a `VoiceLabOutputScope`, as `VoiceLabOutputPlanner.Build` receives them, and returns a `VoiceLabWindow`. `VoiceLabHandles` adds `Minimum`, `Seam` and `CheckTrim`; `VoiceLabAlignment` adds `Words`, both `Trim` overloads and `RelativeWords`; `VoiceLabRoomToneSelection.Select` picks room tone. Refusals are `VoiceLabGeometryException` with a `VoiceLabGeometryErrorCode` and optional word, handle or segment details. The `afterWordId` overload returns `InsertionUnsupported`. `EditingAlgorithms.NormalizeForMatch` is public, with unchanged behaviour. No caller uses these functions yet.
- Round 3 matches the Mac entry (voice-lab.md §8.4, §10.5):
  - Kept source comes from `EditingEngine.FromProject` with the scope's `ActiveClipId`, `EditingAlgorithms.ClipTimes`, `EditingEngine.BuildSegments` and `EditingEngine.BuildEditPlan`, with the window's words and pauses dropped. This replaces round 2's `Place(words, cutWordIds, envelope, sourceSampleRate, durationSeconds, …)` overloads and their `EditingEngine.CutRange` neighbour reach. An editing `ArgumentException` becomes `InvalidInput`.
  - Take edges count kept items only and absorb cut items beyond the window, ending at exactly 0 or `durationSeconds`; in a clip scope only where `ClipTimes` reaches the edge.
  - `SeamFadeCrossesCut` now carries `Incoming`, `Needed` and `Available` instead of a word ID, and the new `WindowTooShort` code carries `Needed` and `Available`. A hard seam's reach is clamped at zero beside overlapping items.
  - `Words` finds lone surrogates by `Rune` decoding, and noncharacters, before `Normalize`, so the refusal never depends on `Normalize` throwing; both are `AlignmentMismatch`. `LoneSurrogatesAreAMismatch` tests it, because the shared JSON cannot carry a lone surrogate.
- Round 4 matches the Mac entry (voice-lab.md §8.4 steps 4 to 6, §10.5, §15 test 3):
  - `Place` selects the scope's enabled replacements as `VoiceLabOutputPlanner.Build` does, with each selected recipe's fades, drops their words and pauses from kept source, removes their synth ranges, and keeps a gap seam clear of their outer fade halves. Replaced words never bridge and block a take edge.
  - The new codes `OverlapsReplacement` and `AdjacentReplacement` carry the replacement in the new `SegmentId` property. They sit after `WindowTooShort` in `VoiceLabGeometryErrorCode`, so the later codes' numeric values moved; nothing stores them.
  - Step 4 now absorbs cut items, events and unlabeled speech wholly inside the window and refuses only a crossing item or a lost kept word. `VoiceLabGeometry.Check` applies the project transcript rule.
  - `VoiceLabLoudness` (wave2-vl-audio-math) and `VoiceLabConform` now read `VoiceLabPatchConstants.VOICED_FRAME_LEVEL` and `RMS_FRAME`; `VoicedFrameDbfs` and its `Math.Pow` call are gone. `VOICED_FRAME_LEVEL` now has one definition on this platform; the 10 ms frame does not. The envelope builders still use their own 0.01 s: `AudioAnalysis`, `EditingAlgorithms` (the empty envelope and both frame lengths), `CacheIndex` and Media's `AudioExtraction`. Each must equal `RMS_FRAME`, and changing `RMS_FRAME` alone would not change how envelopes are built. Those files belong to other tracks.
- Round 5 matches the Mac entry (voice-lab.md §8.4 steps 1, 2 and 6, §10.5, §15 test 3):
  - `Place` checks each replacement's seams and pair rule on the kept pieces with the window and without it, in the new private `SeamLayout`. A check that holds without the window and fails with it is `AdjacentReplacement`. A check that already fails, for example after the pause under a replacement's seam was cut, is judged by prepare's own rule: accepted when it holds with the window, `AdjacentReplacement` when it holds only without, and `InvalidInput` with the replacement in `SegmentId` when the scope did not render before the window. The exception type is unchanged; `SegmentId` is now also set for that `InvalidInput`.
  - When no gap frame fits, a hard seam beside a replacement measures its silence from where that replacement's fade and its own outer half both fit, on both sides, so the sentence right after an accepted one is accepted when the silence allows it.
  - `CutAfterAcceptanceRecipeValidatesAndPrepares` replays the review's reproduction on the passage fixture.
- `Tracks/voice-lab/VoiceLabWindowTests` consumes all 599 shared vectors (sha256 `5c758d8445230581f8455c365d0becea9c1faf2f77814fc6ba3e8797819f60de`), including the 300 property cases and the 150 multi-segment cases. It builds each take's replacements as one-word passage recipes. Every accepted case validates and prepares beside them (64 window vectors, 186 property cases and 63 multi-segment cases): with room tone as a one-word passage recipe, and the 34 window vectors without it as voice-changer recipes. Its six v3 recipe tests pass `VoiceLabProject.Validate` and `VoiceLabRenderContract.Prepare`. They cover gap seams, after the fixture's own replacement is refused with `OverlapsReplacement`; the sentences before and after an accepted replacement; a whole take with `None` seams at both edges; a window beside a cut word; a cut filler absorbed at the take start; and the review's pause cut after an accepted replacement. A short handle is refused with `InvalidFadeHandles`. A scratch harness replayed all 60,515 fuzz placements through this Core, including every final window after the multi-segment fuzz's later edits: every outcome matched the reference, and all 32,183 accepted windows validated and prepared. The public declaration inventory lists this track's types; older `Domain/VoiceLab` types are still missing from it.
- The Release `VoxClip.CrossPlatform.slnf` build (`-nodeReuse:false -p:UseSharedCompilation=false`) had zero warnings and errors. The Core test dll, run directly, passed: 1478 total, 1451 succeeded, 27 existing skips. No VM, provider call or push was used.

### 2026-10-27 — Persisted timestamps round-trip exactly (opus-project-dates)

- `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/Shared/UtcTimestamp.cs` (`Format`, `TryParse`) now backs `UtcTimestampConverter` and project validation (editing §10.1). The converter used to write up to seven trimmed digits (`FFFFFFF`), so the same instant had different text on the two platforms. It read with `DateTimeOffset.TryParse`, which also accepted a lowercase `t`, a space for `T`, missing seconds, a comma before the fraction and 10 or more digits.
- Writes round ticks to the nearest millisecond, an exact half going to the later instant, and always write three digits. `Format` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` past 9999-12-31T23:59:59.999Z. Reads accept 0–9 digits and an uppercase `Z` only, and round the same way, so a loaded value is the one the next save writes and the one the Mac loads. Two stored timestamps therefore compare the same on both platforms, as in `consentVerifiedAt <= provenance.createdAt` and AI-result pruning.
- The same writer now serves cache `meta.json` (`CacheTimestampConverter` still reads any offset, architecture §8.2), `AudioExtraction` metadata and raw transcription entries (`TranscriptionService`, which truncated with `.fff`). It also serves model `installed.json` (`ModelStore`, was the default `+00:00` form), `llm-models.json` `fetchedAt` (`LlmModels`, was `"O"`; its reader now uses the invariant culture), provenance sidecars (Media; `export.createdAt`, `generatedAt` and consent `verifiedAt` were `+00:00` with seven digits) and settings `update.lastCheckAt` (`VelopackUpdates` in the App, was `"O"`). The App's 24-hour check parses that setting with the invariant culture and still reads `"O"` text, and settings validation already accepted both forms. `CliDataJson` reads `--transcript` files through `UtcTimestampConverter`, like the Mac CLI.
- Left alone: log lines (`"O"`); the ElevenLabs clone label, a provider request body sent in whole seconds; HTTP `Retry-After`; `LipsyncJob.SubmittedAt`, a reserved M3 receipt that nothing persists yet; values that are never persisted.
- `shared/fixtures/projects/timestamps.json` holds 602 cases (317 texts, 53 refused texts and 232 instants) and a 10,000-instant SplitMix64 digest. Its SHA-256 is `321a85bda3e68fbc669a07e3d94ae738686b92c6a0012591d9c992401fcf852e`, and `tools/fixtures/projects/generate_timestamps.py` writes it byte-identically on every run. `tests/VoxClip.Core.Tests/TimestampWireRuleTests.cs` replays every case through `UtcTimestamp`, the converter, `ProjectValidation` and `ProjectSerialization`, checks the digest, and saves every example project twice.
- The example projects and the Voice Lab examples still store whole seconds, so their first save writes `.000`. `TimestampWireRuleTests` lists them, and `SharedContractTests`, `ProjectStoreTests` and `VoiceLabUpgradeTests` compare saves with the examples' timestamps written in canonical form (`TimestampWireRuleTests.CanonicalTimestamps`).
- `TranscriptionService` never overwrites a paid ElevenLabs entry. When the cache file exists but does not read as this request's entry, `SetAsideUnreadableCache` moves it to `<name>.unreadable-<unix ms>` before the recovery write or the upload whose response would replace it, never replacing an existing file. If the move fails, transcription stops with the cache-unavailable error. The entry reader never parses `createdAt`, so its format cannot make an entry unreadable. Tests: `UnreadableEntryIsSetAsideBeforeANewUpload` and `ForeignCreatedAtStillServesThePaidResponse` (`PaidCacheSafetyTests`).
- `CacheTimestampConverter` rounds what it reads as a write does (internal `UtcTimestamp.TryRound`) and refuses an instant that rounds past 9999-12-31T23:59:59.999Z. Such a crafted or corrupt `meta.json` used to read, then make `ExtractAudioAsync`, `TouchAsync` and `EvictAsync` throw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` when they rewrote it. It is now corrupt metadata: a cache miss that extraction rewrites and eviction removes. Test: `CacheEnvelopeTests.MetadataPastTheLastMillisecondIsAMissNotACrash`.
- Tests pin the new writer text: the sidecar's `export.createdAt` from a sub-millisecond `DateTimeOffset` (62.5 ms, written `.063Z`), `aiSegments[].generatedAt` and `consent[].verifiedAt` (Media `VoiceLabDisclosureTests`); `installed.json` `installedAt` (`ModelStoreTests`), `llm-models.json` `fetchedAt` (`HttpConnectorTests`, exact text) and the raw entry's `createdAt`; and `update.lastCheckAt` in both forms (`SettingsStoreTests`). The inventory below lists `UtcTimestamp` and `TimestampWireRuleTests`.
- Compatibility: Windows builds from before 2026-10-27 read the new markers and raw entries, because they never parse `installedAt` or the raw `createdAt`. macOS builds from before that date treat them as missing (see the macOS ledger).
- Verification after merging main `ca4064f8`, on macOS: `dotnet build VoxClip.CrossPlatform.slnf -c Release -nodeReuse:false -p:UseSharedCompilation=false` passed with 0 warnings. The Core test dll ran 1,474 tests: 0 failed, 27 skipped (live-provider and Windows-only prerequisites). The Media test dll, without the `RequiresFFmpeg` and `RequiresWindows` traits, ran 472: 0 failed, 4 skipped. `VoxClip.App` is WinUI and builds only on Windows, so hosted CI compiles the `VelopackUpdates` edit.

Baseline: **2026-10-25**. This is the handoff boundary for parallel implementers, not permission to redesign neighboring tracks. All paths below are relative to `apps/windows/`. Public source declarations are real C# types; their XML documentation cites the owning spec. The initial inventory contains 304 public declarations across 52 source files, including nested union cases and test-support types. Internal CLI wiring is separately owned by win-media.

## Ownership rules

| Track | Exclusive implementation folders |
|---|---|
| win-editing | `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/Editing/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Project/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Settings/` |
| win-speech | `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/Transcript/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Speech/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Models/`, `src/VoxClip.Media/Speech/` |
| win-captions | `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/Captions/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Captions/`, `src/VoxClip.Media/Captions/` |
| win-ai | `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/Clips/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/LLM/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Process/` |
| win-media | `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Media/`, `src/VoxClip.Media/Ffmpeg/`, `src/VoxClip.Media/Pipeline/`, `src/VoxClip.Media/Audio/`, `src/VoxClip.Cli/`, `tests/VoxClip.Media.Tests/` |
| win-ui | `src/VoxClip.App/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Logging/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Updates/`, `src/VoxClip.Media/Thumbnails/`, `tests/VoxClip.Media.Tests/Thumbnails/` |
| share-core | `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/Share/`, `src/VoxClip.Core/Services/Share/`, `tests/VoxClip.Core.Tests/Tracks/share-core/` |
| skeleton / lead | `src/VoxClip.Core/Domain/Shared/`, solution/build/package configuration, shared test support and common contract tests, this inventory |

- Update the existing stub **in place**; do not create alternate implementations or backup source trees. A type's owner controls its implementation, not unilateral breaking changes to public contracts.
- Each track owns its corresponding `tests/VoxClip.Core.Tests/Tracks/*PlaceholderTests.cs`. Add further track tests under a matching `Tracks/<track>/` folder to avoid collisions. Replace skip placeholders with conformance coverage when the implementation is ready.
- No track edits another track's folders without agreement. Project files, central packages, shared value types, `ProjectJsonContext`, the common test fixture loader and common test classes require lead coordination. New source files compile through SDK globs; do not edit csproj files to list ordinary C# files.
- `Domain/Shared` is deliberately frozen common value code, owned by the skeleton/lead. Project persistence details remain schema-driven. `Services/Media` holds media-free boundaries in Core, not native media implementations. Core has no third-party packages or UI/native dependencies.
- Never edit `doc/`, `shared/` or `apps/macos/` as part of a Windows implementation track. Runtime assets remain linked from `shared/`, not copied into source. Separate platform contracts share behavior through fixtures, not native code.

## How to propose a contract change

1. Append a **dated proposal** to the Change ledger below: proposed signature/type/JSON delta, owning track, affected consumers, spec section, migration/fixture impact and rationale.
2. Ask the lead and affected owners to agree before editing public declarations. A proposal is not approval; explicitly record the agreed decision and date.
3. Land producer, consumer, generated serialization metadata and test changes together. Rebuild the five-project cross-platform solution and both WinUI RIDs. Never silently rename fields, change enum wire values, remove cancellation or change ownership.
4. Update this inventory for every added/removed public type; use fully qualified nested case names. Serialized types must have source-generated metadata, never a reflection-only JsonSerializer call in AOT-compatible Core.

## Concurrency and lifecycle boundary

Long-running service contracts take `CancellationToken` and nullable `IProgress<T>` where meaningful. Pure domain math and per-frame allocation-free draw calls are synchronous; draw/compose still accept cancellation. Async resource owners use `IAsyncDisposable`. Consumers hand off immutable snapshots; they never share FFmpeg contexts across threads. UI owners dispatch progress/state changes to the UI thread. Preview and export both call `IFrameComposer.Compose`, with exactly one caption rasterizer. Finalizers may release handles only, never perform native owner-thread operations.

Stub async work throws `VoxClipException(NotImplemented, "<track-id>")`; safe startup/status placeholders return documented empty/unavailable states and do no I/O. Their absence of an exception does **not** mean a feature is implemented. `NotImplemented` is a skeleton-only code and must not ship as a new platform behavior without an agreed spec change. Native, HTTP and subprocess failures eventually map to the stable architecture §10.2 codes; logs contain redacted metadata, never provider bodies, prompts, transcript text, raw paths or keys.

## Persisted and runtime boundaries

- `Project`/`TimedWord`/`Transcript` mirror the final project schema, including required export choices, `share.captions`, clip `selected` and empty `voiceLab.segments`. The stored transcript and its timings never change during editing. `ProjectSerialization` provides strict generated decoding/basic invariants; full store cleanup, relink and atomic I/O remain win-editing work.
- `ExportPreset`/`FrameRate`/`ReframeSettings` are **runtime resolved output types**, not custom presets stored in a v1 project. Stored `ProjectExport` has only aspect, quality, SRT and folder.
- `AppSettings` preserves a **flat dotted-key** settings object through extension data, including unknown keys and `llm.model.<connector>`; there is no serialized `values` wrapper. Treat loaded mutable JSON collections as owned snapshots, not shared mutable state.
- `EngineRef` is the single stored and raw engine metadata type (plan W1), with `TranscriptEngine` wire values and nullable model/locale fields written in both JSON contexts.
- Caption schema records and enum wire values are declared in `CaptionStyle.cs`; runtime layout is in `CaptionContracts.cs`. Schema `CaptionLayout` is not a second caption engine. The rasterizer consumes bundled font glyph outlines; `DrawText`/system-font fallback is forbidden.
- LLM JSON uses `RawClip` double indices (`start_word`, `end_word`) until per-item validation. Clip finding returns `AiResult`; the editor stores it and executes `AddAiClips` as one undo step. The service never silently changes edits or writes a project.
- Voice Lab Core now has explicit regional ElevenLabs discovery, consent-policy inputs, prepared-action approval, single-dispatch clone/TTS/conversion operations, and inert LLM rewrite proposals. Consent recording, calibrated speaker verification, media/timeline persistence, UI, account linking and project source replacement are not implemented; schema-v1 voice segments stay empty.

## Spec reconciliations and limitations (2026-10-25)

1. **Transcript shape:** transcription §3.1 uses a compact transcript/engine enum and broader timing options, while architecture §3, editing §10 and `project.schema.json` define the portable expanded transcript with an engine metadata object and explicit per-word provenance. Persistence follows the latter; adapter routing and raw timing types remain separate. Persisted `estimated` timing is rejected, as in the project schema.
2. **Speech interface:** the transcription C# sketch omits Swift `prepare` and `needsSystemAssets`. Both are present in the cross-platform contract; Windows never implements Apple Speech, but can decode catalogue entries.
3. **Preview caption size:** architecture §6.3 describes composition at preview size; captions requires stable export-resolution layout. The contract keeps canonical export-pixel caption geometry and lets the single compose path rasterize it at a uniform preview scale. It does not introduce preview-specific reflow or a second renderer. Architecture wins on threading, module placement and output target size.
4. **Inspector tabs:** ADR-R12 describes M1 Captions/Clips only; the skeleton brief explicitly requests an AI tab. The extra tab is a disabled placeholder, not an M1 feature. No Voice Lab controls or implementation are exposed.
5. **Errors:** `NotImplemented` is explicitly required by the skeleton brief but absent from architecture §10.2. It is the sole added error code, maps to exit 1, and is documented as non-shipping. `MediaException` carries safe native return metadata while remaining a `VoxClipException`.
6. **Test runner:** the latest stable NuGet `xunit.v3` package is 4.0.2 and uses Microsoft.Testing.Platform v2. .NET 10 needs the `global.json` runner opt-in and `dotnet test --project ...`; the architecture's older positional project command is not compatible. The category filter expression is unchanged.
7. **Dependencies:** the user requires SkiaSharp 3.x and two exact Microsoft SDK pins, which override unqualified latest wording. NAudio.Wasapi 22.0.0 is unlisted, not the selected stable version. Whisper.net.Runtime includes win-arm64 and win-x64 CPU assets; NoAvx adds the x64 CPU fallback. FFmpeg.AutoGen 9.0.1.1 is paired with the self-built pinned FFmpeg n9.0.2 LGPL shared ABI; the separate BtbN archive in `testTools` is never shipped.
8. **Concurrent schema refinements:** during this handoff, shared contracts changed to simplified stored export choices, required `share.captions` and clip `selected`, flat settings and automatic addition of AI result ranges. This skeleton follows those current schemas/specs, not the older custom-export-preset/accept-only drafts. Current architecture §9 also adds `--audio-out` and the three caption rendering forms; the CLI includes all of them.
9. **Environment:** the VM has no WinUI template/global winapp tool. The checked-in scaffold builds directly with the pinned SDK; no global tooling or other VM folders were changed. FFmpeg binaries, real decoder/license loading, media conformance and release packaging are future track work. Compilation is not a claim of complete product behavior.
10. **Globalization:** the current architecture §2.3 explicitly requires ICU-backed NFC, overriding the older draft's invariant-globalization setting. `Directory.Build.props` matches the current block verbatim, leaves invariant mode unset, and a regression test checks decomposed-to-composed normalization. Cross-platform determinism uses explicit ordinal/casemap/invariant-number APIs, not runtime invariant mode.

## Public declaration inventory

Round 4 additions: `Services/Speech/LanguageRouter.cs` declares public `LanguageRouter` (plan W5); `Services/Speech/SpeechTransport.cs` declares public `ImportProgress`.

Round 6 helper boundary: `Services/Speech/SpeechCopy.cs` declares public `SpeechCopy`, including `SpeechCopy.ValidateIdentifiers(TranscriptionPlan)`; `Services/Speech/SpeechTransport.cs` declares public `SpeechProgress<T>`. Only the copy, progress and catalogue helpers required by the native Whisper adapter are public; Media no longer has access to Core internals.

Round 7 lead-approved W5 composition change: `ElevenLabsTranscriber` exposes only `ElevenLabsTranscriber()` and `ElevenLabsTranscriber(IKeyProvider, ElevenLabsRegion = Default, ModelCatalog? = null, ILog? = null)`. No public constructor accepts a client, handler or host. The sole test injection operation is internal `ForTesting(IKeyProvider, HttpMessageHandler, ElevenLabsRegion, ModelCatalog? = null, ILog? = null)`; it requires a non-null handler. All shared constructors are private.

Every listed declaration maps to its containing file and one owner. Nested union cases are prefixed with their parent type. Names are namespace-relative; the file declares the exact namespace. Test classes are included for completeness but are not production APIs.
